<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Worktrees
- Prefer reusing a suitable active worktree. Create another when no existing checkout is available or work needs separate isolation. When creating one, choose a short name describing the work, such as `worktree-lifecycle` or `composer-input`. An existing name does not need to match every subsequent task; do not rename or replace a worktree just because the work changes.
- Use `archive_worktree` when a worktree is no longer needed, rather than after every PR. Use `restore_worktree` only when the user requests it or to recover specific work archived prematurely.
- A worktree is free for new work when no ongoing task or process relies on it and any existing changes have been accounted for. Prepare the appropriate branch and base before starting new work. Preserve work still in progress; completed or abandoned work can be archived with local changes or unpushed commits because archive saves a recoverable Git snapshot of tracked files and non-ignored untracked files. Ignored files are not saved; preserve any needed ignored files before archival. Do not delete files just to make a worktree eligible for archival. Preserve pinned, shared, or in-use worktrees when cleaning up. Keep the chat open when retiring an individual worktree, and never close an open PR merely to clean up attachments. Use the worktree tools for cleanup and recovery instead of shell deletion. Check at these lifecycle transitions, not every turn.
- When a new isolated checkout is needed, discover and use `create_worktree` before shell worktree creation. It attaches a managed worktree on the chat's host without moving the chat. Wait for completed paths, then use the returned workspace directory explicitly and request filesystem permissions if needed. Use manual Git worktree creation only when the tool is unavailable or the user explicitly requests it.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\r\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\r\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\r\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\r\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\r\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\r\n- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\r\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\r\n\r\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\r\n\r\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\r\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\r\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\r\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.\r\n- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.\r\n- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\r\n'}]

Exact paths only;\r\n# the patterns stay full-strength and any new path still fails.\r\ncontent_allowlist=(\r\n  # "don\'t do this" documentation examples, not live credentials\r\n  \'skills/claude/higgsfield-websites/references/security.md\'\r\n  \'skills/codex/higgsfield-websites/references/security.md\'\r\n)\r\n\r\nscan_blob() {\r\n  local label="$1"\r\n  local plain="${label#history:}"\r\n  if (( ${content_allowlist[(Ie)$plain]} )); then\r\n    cat >/dev/null\r\n    return 0\r\n  fi\r\n  if LC_ALL=C /usr/bin/grep -EIna "$secret_re" 2>/dev/null; then\r\n    print -u2 "Potential secret rejected in: $label"\r\n    return 1\r\n  fi\r\n}\r\n\r\ncase "$mode" in\r\n  staged)\r\n    git diff --cached --name-only --diff-filter=ACMR | scan_names\r\n    while IFS= read -r filepath; do\r\n      [[ -z "$filepath" ]] && continue\r\n      git show ":$filepath" 2>/dev/null | scan_blob "$filepath"\r\n    done < <(git diff --cached --name-only --diff-filter=ACMR)\r\n    ;;\r\n  worktree)\r\n    git ls-files | scan_names\r\n    while IFS= read -r filepath; do\r\n      [[ -f "$filepath" ]] || continue\r\n      /usr/bin/file -b --mime "$filepath" | /usr/bin/grep -q \'charset=binary\' && continue\r\n      scan_blob "$filepath" < "$filepath"\r\n    done < <(git ls-files)\r\n    ;;\r\n  history)\r\n    git rev-list --objects --all | git cat-file --batch-check=\'%(objecttype) %(objectname) %(rest)\' |\r\n      while IFS=\' \' read -r type oid filepath; There is no `private/` tier, no macOS Keychain, no encrypted vault — the old `records.sparsebundle` design is dead, not deferred (decided 2026-07-17).\r\n- **Passwords, bank/card data, and government IDs are out of scope for this OS entirely.** Joshua keeps them outside it. Never ask him to paste one, never store one, never write one to `.env`.\r\n- **Everything else is ordinary local-only working material** and may live in this repository: names, emails, schedules, application essays, score reports, profiles, session transcripts and teaching notes, correspondence, and shareable `buy.stripe.com` payment links. Classify by what the content *is*, never by a filename.\r\n- **Safety comes from `.claude/settings.json`** (allow/deny tool lists) and scoped API keys, not from prompt instructions. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.\r\n\r\nAs of 2026-07-17 this OS stores no secret material beyond API keys in the root `.env`. The earlier design's private stores (`private/records.sparsebundle`, migration manifests, `private/records/`, `private/config/`) were all verified empty and removed. Do not recreate them.\r\n\r\n## Student data\r\n\r\nOne folder per student under `students/<name>/` holds their whole working record: transcripts, correspondence, homework, notes, profile, and payment-link pages. Open the folder and everything is there. There is no separate private student tier — every file was read and found to contain no secret material.\r\n\r\nThe tradeoff is accepted deliberately: **outer Git history retains student data permanently.** Since this brain has no Git remote, that is fine locally. The scrub-before-remote rule below is the compensating control, and it is load-bearing.\r\n\r\n## Before any remote or push\r\n\r\n1. Stop: this repository is intentionally local-only.\r\n2. Scan the complete outer worktree and Git history, every retained nested repository and its history, and ignored runtime paths.\r\n3. Confirm `.env`, logs, locks, and `projects/lsatu/` are untracked, and that no credential exists in reachable or dangling Git objects.\r\n4. **Scrub student data.** Student material — names, transcripts, correspondence, payment-link pages — is tracked by design and is therefore in history permanently. It is not publishable as-is. Decide per destination whether it is redacted, filtered out of history, or whether the remote is private enough to carry it. Skipping this step publishes every student's working record.\r\n5. Obtain Joshua's explicit approval before adding any remote or pushing anything.\r\n"}]

Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\r\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\r\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\r\ntype: operating-layer\r\nsubject: Lovable Cloud (LSAT U backend platform)\r\nupdated: 2026-07-19\r\naliases: []\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Lovable Cloud (LSAT U backend platform)\r\n\r\nSource: [[Lovable Cloud|Lovable Cloud docs clipping]] (`raw/sources/clips/lovable-cloud/`) (docs.lovable.dev/integrations/cloud, ingested 2026-07-19). This is what runs the LSAT U production backend (`atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju.supabase.co`).\r\n\r\n## What it is\r\n\r\nLovable Cloud is Lovable\'s managed backend: a Supabase-based stack (Postgres, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, scheduled Jobs, AI gateway) that Lovable itself provisions and operates. The project is **not** in Joshua\'s own Supabase org and has no supabase.com dashboard — everything is managed through the Lovable editor\'s Cloud tab or by prompting Lovable in chat.\r\n\r\n## Capabilities that matter for LSAT U\r\n\r\n- **Database**: schema changes via SQL migrations that *Lovable* applies (pushed migrations do not auto-apply — confirmed 2026-07-19 when two booking migrations sat unapplied while edge functions synced). Daily backups, ~14 days retained, restorable from Cloud tab (restore is destructive/permanent).\r\n- **Storage**: private-by-default buckets, files up to 2 GB. Public buckets blocked by default (workspace setting). Files are NOT included in DB exports — downloaded separately from Cloud tab → Storage.\r\n- **Edge Functions**: deployed automatically on git push (this part does auto-sync). Logs visible in Cloud tab.\r\n- **Jobs**: cron-style scheduled tasks, created/changed via SQL or Lovable chat.\r\n- **Auth**: email/phone/Google/Apple; settings managed in Cloud tab.\r\n- **Secrets**: write-only store for backend keys, injected into edge functions. `SUPABASE_*` and `LOVABLE_*` are reserved/auto-managed; the service-role key exists in the edge-function env (`Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")`) but is never shown to the user.\r\n- **`.env` rule**: `VITE_*` values live in the committed `.env` (must NOT be gitignored in the lsatu repo — breaks Lovable previews); backend secrets live in the Secrets UI, never in `.env`.\r\n- **Ops via chat**: health checks, slow-query analysis + auto-indexing, instance resize (Tiny→Large with approval card).\r\n- **Billing**: one credit balance; Cloud + AI usage = Run credits; 20-credit monthly Cloud grant + 4-credit AI grant on paid plans. Auto-pause on inactivity (scheduled jobs prevent auto-pause).\r\n\r\n## Control boundaries (Jay 2 vs Lovable)\r\n\r\nJay 2 can reach the backend only through: the deployed edge functions (admin JWT via `LSATPREP_ADMIN_*` creds), the public REST/auth APIs (anon key), and the git repo (code + migrations that Lovable then deploys/applies).\r\n\r\n**Lovable-UI-only** (no API path, but Joshua can drive manually): the **Cloud tab → SQL editor** runs arbitrary SQL including DDL — discovered 2026-07-19 — so pending migrations never actually block on Lovable chat: Jay 2 writes the SQL, Joshua pastes and Runs. Still UI-only with no external path: secrets management, storage browsing/download, backups/restore, instance sizing, auth provider config, pause/remove, DB exports. There is no service-role key or DB connection string available outside Lovable, by design.\r\n\r\n**Escape hatches**: full DB export (≤5 GB, 1/day) + GitHub repo = migration path to a self-owned Supabase project, but Lovable offers no one-click transfer, storage/auth/secrets must be rebuilt by hand, and Cloud→Supabase switching of a live project is explicitly unsupported. Decision 2026-07-19: stay on Lovable Cloud; treat Lovable chat as the operator for DB-level actions ("apply the pending migrations", "run a health check").\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'wiki/lovable-cloud.md:3:subject: Lovable Cloud (LSAT U backend platform)\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:8:# Lovable Cloud (LSAT U backend platform)\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:10:Source: [[Lovable Cloud|Lovable Cloud docs clipping]] (`raw/sources/clips/lovable-cloud/`) (docs.lovable.dev/integrations/cloud, ingested 2026-07-19). This is what runs the LSAT U production backend (`atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju.supabase.co`).\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:14:Lovable Cloud is Lovable\'s managed backend: a Supabase-based stack (Postgres, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, scheduled Jobs, AI gateway) that Lovable itself provisions and operates. The project is **not** in Joshua\'s own Supabase org and has no supabase.com dashboard — everything is managed through the Lovable editor\'s Cloud tab or by prompting Lovable in chat.\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:18:- **Database**: schema changes via SQL migrations that *Lovable* applies (pushed migrations do not auto-apply — confirmed 2026-07-19 when two booking migrations sat unapplied while edge functions synced). Daily backups, ~14 days retained, restorable from Cloud tab (restore is destructive/permanent).\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:20:- **Edge Functions**: deployed automatically on git push (this part does auto-sync). Logs visible in Cloud tab.\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:21:- **Jobs**: cron-style scheduled tasks, created/changed via SQL or Lovable chat.\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:23:- **Secrets**: write-only store for backend keys, injected into edge functions. `SUPABASE_*` and `LOVABLE_*` are reserved/auto-managed; the service-role key exists in the edge-function env (`Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")`) but is never shown to the user.\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:24:- **`.env` rule**: `VITE_*` values live in the committed `.env` (must NOT be gitignored in the lsatu repo — breaks Lovable previews); backend secrets live in the Secrets UI, never in `.env`.\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:28:## Control boundaries (Jay 2 vs Lovable)\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:30:Jay 2 can reach the backend only through: the deployed edge functions (admin JWT via `LSATPREP_ADMIN_*` creds), the public REST/auth APIs (anon key), and the git repo (code + migrations that Lovable then deploys/applies).\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:32:**Lovable-UI-only** (no API path, but Joshua can drive manually): the **Cloud tab → SQL editor** runs arbitrary SQL including DDL — discovered 2026-07-19 — so pending migrations never actually block on Lovable chat: Jay 2 writes the SQL, Joshua pastes and Runs. Still UI-only with no external path: secrets management, storage browsing/download, backups/restore, instance sizing, auth provider config, pause/remove, DB exports. There is no service-role key or DB connection string available outside Lovable, by design.\r\nwiki/lovable-cloud.md:34:**Escape hatches**: full DB export (≤5 GB, 1/day) + GitHub repo = migration path to a self-owned Supabase project, but Lovable offers no one-click transfer, storage/auth/secrets must be rebuilt by hand, and Cloud→Supabase switching of a live project is explicitly unsupported. Decision 2026-07-19: stay on Lovable Cloud; treat Lovable chat as the operator for DB-level actions ("apply the pending migrations", "run a health check").\r\nwiki/dashboard.md:15:| Homework, dossier, publish state | LSAT U Supabase |\nwiki/dashboard.md:18:| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` (own repo, own instructions) |\nwiki/dashboard.md:21:Product status detail lives in the app and `projects/lsatu/docs/`; the old dashboard snapshot is archived at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/dashboard.md`.\nprojects/lsatu/README.md:3:Premium ed-tech web app: React 18 + Vite 5 + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui, with a Supabase backend (Edge Functions), hosted on Lovable.\r\nprojects/lsatu/README.md:9:- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\r\nwiki/hot.md:14:- Round 8 of the RRC demo films ("text written in the air") is live on lsatprep.study under Lovable deployment `d0ecbeb4`: the dark slabs are gone, every card is light-written type on the room, phrases write in under a second, natural blinks on holds. App PR #122 merged as `6836bdb` (six files, no code); film source in private repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-films` (`main` = R8 `6357e00`, R7 tagged `r7-shipped-pub`).\nwiki/hot.md:16:- Found while verifying: **Lovable static hosting ignores HTTP Range** for every asset. Chrome reports `video.seekable [0,0]`, so the theatre\'s scrubber and arrow keys do nothing on production, nothing is cached, and iPhone playback is at risk (Apple requires byte-range support; not tested on a device). Predates Round 8; the bootcamp mp4 has it too. Fix is a public Supabase Storage bucket plus a repoint in the demos file. Needs his go.\nwiki/hot.md:20:- Rollback if rejected: `git checkout 9b6b51c -- public/rrc-demos` on a fresh branch, PR, merge, `deploy_project`.\nwiki/hot.md:28:- Housekeeping owed: `scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts` has not been run since 2026-09-09; `sweep-rrc-grading` is still unscheduled; merged `rrc-step-6`/`-7` branches undeleted; a leftover empty directory `projects/lsatu-wt-rrc12` could not be removed (permission denied) and needs a manual delete.\nwiki/hot.md:33:- **D1 #5 (post-session pipeline): DEPLOYED and live, 2026-07-16.** Lovable pass reported green (3 functions, migration present, all secrets — `EDGE_INTERNAL_KEY` is project-scoped so the same-value trap can\'t happen; gateway `ai.gateway.lovable.dev` confirmed unchanged). Frontend Publish verified live in the browser. **Then Joshua pivoted the workflow: the app\'s ingest UI is not how he\'ll work — Claude is the workplace; ingestion and post-session actions go through Claude directly.** The formal runbook E2E was aborted at his direction and is no longer the plan of record. **The `lsat-u-platform-actions` skill now exists (activated both runtimes)** — one entry point to assign drills/homework/activities, post to classrooms, message students, and ingest sessions from this workspace via his Chrome admin session. The original "D1 #6 gated on live E2E" adjudication predates this pivot and needs re-adjudication.\n'}]

  # CLAUDE.md

  This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

  ## Project snapshot

  LSAT U is a premium ed-tech web app: React 18 + Vite 5 + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui, Supabase backend with Edge Functions, hosted on Lovable. The Lovable-connected project is the source of truth; GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`) is the mirror. Treat the connected project as canonical unless told otherwise.

  ## Second brain (product memory)

  Product context, design decisions, pedagogy, and status do NOT live in this repo. They live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`. This repo is the code; the vault is the decision layer. Read the vault when starting a session, needing product/design/pedagogy context, or making a decision that touches roadmap or UX. Skip it for trivial code edits.

  Path sanity check first:

  - Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
  - Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`

  If any older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale \u2014 the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

  Protocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:

  - **`/pickup`** (start / "brief me"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.
  - **`/wrapup`** (handoff / "done for today"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.

  `wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.

  Where LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):

  The vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:
  ```

  # AGENTS.md

  This file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.

  ## Fast Locator

  - Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
  - Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`

  - Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`

  If an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale \u2014 the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

  ## Project Map

  - `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.
  - `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.
  - `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.
  - `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.
  - `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.
  - `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.
  - `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.
  - `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.

  ## Stack And Commands

  - Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.
  - Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.
  - Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.
  - Build: `npm run build`
  ```

## user

Task: prepare everything a Claude Code cloud session needs to work on LSAT U with full access. Read AGENTS.md in the ai-os repo root first. Run one command per call. Run with full access.

HARD RULES
- Never print, copy, log, or commit the VALUE of any secret, token, key, password, or .env entry. Names only.
- Do NOT edit or push anything in the LSAT U app repo (projects/lsatu/). A push to its main auto-deploys to production.
- No em-dashes in anything you write.
- Do not touch raw/. Do not create gitlinks.

STEP 1. Gather facts (read-only)
In projects/lsatu/ report:
a) Node version in use (node -v) and the package manager (npm, package-lock.json is canonical).
b) The exact commands for: install, typecheck, build, dev server, tests, lint. Read package.json scripts.
c) Every environment variable NAME the app, the scripts/ folder, and supabase/functions/ read. Include names from .env.example, .env (names only), import.meta.env.*, process.env.*, and Deno.env.get(...). For each name say what it is for and whether the browser or a script or an Edge Function uses it.
d) Which scripts need network access or credentials (for example the LSAT Lab sync scripts behind the lsat-lab-sync skill, the Supabase queries, Google API calls). List script path, what it does, and which env var names it needs.
e) Supabase: project ref, list of Edge Function folders, count of migrations, and whether a read-only database role or read-only key exists. If not, say so.

STEP 2. Write the setup file
Create references/cloud-session-setup.md in the ai-os repo containing:
1. A "Secrets to add" table with columns: NAME, purpose, who uses it, where Joshua finds the value (for example Lovable Cloud dashboard, Supabase settings, Google Cloud console). NO values.
2. A ready-to-paste "Setup script" block for the Claude Code cloud environment that runs from the app repo: install dependencies with npm ci, and nothing that needs a secret. Keep it under 15 lines.
3. The verified commands from step 1b.
4. A "Recommended read-only access" section: whether a read-only Supabase key or role can be created for the cloud session, and the steps to create it. Do not create it yourself.
5. A "Not available in the cloud" list: local-only things (gws CLI keyring, ~/.config/gws, Claude native memory, raw/data-about-me, the Windows-only tools).

STEP 3. Commit and push
Stage only references/cloud-session-setup.md. Commit message: "Add cloud session setup reference (names only)". Run git fetch origin, then git pull --rebase or merge if main moved, then git push origin main. Verify git status -sb shows main up to date with origin/main.

FINAL REPORT
Give: the commit hash, the full list of secret NAMES needed, any script you could not verify and why, and anything that needs Joshua to act in a browser.

Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.\r\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\r\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\r\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\r\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\r\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\r\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\r\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\r\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\r\n- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\r\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\r\n\r\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\r\n\r\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\r\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\r\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\r\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.\r\n- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.\r\n- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 8.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Import-Clixml: Root element is missing.\r\nInvalidOperation: Index operation failed; the array index evaluated to null.\r\nInvalidOperation: Index operation failed; the array index evaluated to null.\r\n---\r\nname: lsat-lab-sync\r\ndescription: Pull LSAT Lab\'s data in bulk and audit or sync the LSAT U LR question bank and database against it, with scripts instead of clicking through pages. Use when Joshua says "pull from LSAT Lab", "sync with LSAT Lab", "tag these questions", "check the bank against LSAT Lab", "are any answer keys wrong", "is anything misfiled", "which explanations does LSAT Lab have that we don\'t", "full scan", or after any import into public/data/PT*-LR.json or the lr_* tables. Covers difficulty, reasoning type, features, answer keys, question text, choice text, missing slots and explanation coverage.\r\n---\r\n\r\n# LSAT Lab sync\r\n\r\nEverything lives in `system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/` in the vault; its `README.md` has the endpoint details. Run the LSAT U steps from an LSAT U checkout on its own branch (never the main checkout another session may be using), and ship bank changes as a PR.\r\n\r\n## Two sources, pick by need\r\n\r\n| Need | Source | Login |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| Difficulty, question type, features, reasoning type for all 10,171 LSAT Lab questions | `app.lsatlab.com/my/services/get-questions-just-props.php` + `/api/question-properties`, fetched inside a logged-in tab | Yes |\r\n| Answer key, answer %, trap labels, written explanation, question text, first words of each choice, each test\'s question list | public pages `www.lsatlab.com/lsat-explanations/PT<n>-S<s>-Q<q>` via `crawl_public.py` | No |\r\n\r\nFull answer-choice text is on neither (public pages cut choices short). Choice text comes from the official PrepTest PDFs Joshua keeps in Downloads.\r\n\r\n## 1. Refresh the logged-in data (only when tags may have changed)\r\n\r\nUse the Claude Code browser (in-app pane; Joshua prefers it, and its JS results come back whole). Joshua must already be logged in to `app.lsatlab.com`; never enter his password. Run the two snippets in the README with the browser JavaScript tool: `properties.json` comes back inline; the big question list downloads to Downloads, then move it to `data/questions-props.json`.\r\n\r\n## 2. Crawl the public pages\r\n\r\n```bash\r\npython <vault>/system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/crawl_public.py            # every LR slot in the bank\r\npython <vault>/system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/crawl_public.py --pts 113  # one test\r\n```\r\n~4,000 pages, 6 workers, resumable (skips slots already in `data/public/`). Run it in the background. Delete `data/public/` to force a refresh.\r\n\r\n## 3. Pull the database (read-only)\r\n\r\n```bash\r\npython system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/db_pull.py   # from the vault root; writes data/db/\r\n```\r\n\r\n## 4. Audit\r\n\r\n```bash\r\npython <vault>/system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/audit_bank.py --db <vault>/system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/data/db --out <report.md>\r\n```\r\nFindings: `key_mismatch` (bank / database / LSAT Lab disagree), `stimulus_not_on_lsatlab` / `stem_not_on_lsatlab` / `choice_not_on_lsatlab` (wrong or damaged question text), `db_stimulus_not_on_lsatlab`, `in_lsatlab_not_in_bank` / `in_bank_not_in_lsatlab_list` (missing or extra slots), `lsatlab_explained_lsatu_not` (LSAT Lab has a written explanation where LSAT U\'s Coverage State is not Complete), `labels_differ`.\r\n\r\nRead a sample of every finding type before reporting it. Text checks compare normalized prefixes, so a hit can be a formatting difference; say which you confirmed.\r\n\r\n## 5. Fix\r\n\r\n- **Labels:** `tag_bank.py --slots ... | --slots-file ... | --missing`, then `node scripts/build-question-bank.mjs`. LSAT Lab\'s current tags win (Joshua, 2026-09-27). Never touches question type.\r\n- **Wrong question in a slot:** the database text is usually right. Confirm on the LSAT Lab page, then rebuild the slot: stimulus + stem + key from the database, choices from the PDF. See `corpus/lr-explanations/fixes/` for the 2026-09-27 repair.\r\n- **Answer keys:** a disagreement goes to Joshua before anything changes ("I think this question is marked wrong"), with the three sources side by side.\r\n- **Explanations:** database edits go through a snapshot in `raw/sources/lsatu-lr-snapshots/` first, then Lovable `query_database`.\r\n- Ship: PR, merge when Joshua says, then `deploy_project` (free) and confirm the change on lsatprep.study by fetching the file.\r\n\r\n## Record\r\n\r\nAppend findings to `corpus/lr-explanations/fixes/index.md` and a line to `wiki/log.md`.\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Securing your data | Supabase Docs (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/secure-data)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search0\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 days ago; Crawled: 3 days ago;   * Turn on Row Level Security (RLS) for your tables and properly configure your access policies to grant the least privileges necessary for your app to function ... Unlike your publishable key, your secret and service role keys are never safe to expose because they bypass RLS. ... Supabase and Postgres provide you with multiple ways to manage security, including but not limited to Row Level Security.\nYou can disable the Data API if your app only uses direct connections.\n\n## Frontend access#\n\nFor frontend apps, the Data API is the usual choice. You can keep your data secure while accessing it from the frontend, so long as you:\n\n  * Turn on Row Level Security (RLS) for your tables and properly configure your access policies to grant the least privileges necessary for your app to function\n  * Use your Supabase publishable key when you create a Supabase client\n\nYour publishable key is safe to expose with RLS enabled, because row access permission is checked against your access policies and the user\'s JSON Web Token (JWT). The JWT is automatically sent by the Supabase client libraries if the user is signed in using Supabase Auth.\n\nOlder projects may also show an `anon` key. Treat it like a publishable key: it can identify your project, but it is not a secret and must be paired with RLS and least-privilege grants.\n\nNever expose your service role or secret keys on the frontend\n\nUnlike your publishable key, your secret and service role keys are never safe to expose because they bypass RLS. Only use your secret and service role keys on the backend. Treat them as secrets (for example, import them as sensitive environment variables instead of hardcoding them).\n\n## More information#\n\nSupabase and Postgres provide you with multiple ways to manage security, including but not limited to Row Level Security. See the Access and Security pages for more information:\n\n  * Row Level Security\n  * Column Level Security\n  * Securing your API\n  * Managing Postgres roles\n  * Managing secrets with Vault--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nRow Level Security | Supabase Docs (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/postgres/row-level-security)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search1\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 days ago; Crawled: 3 days ago; Using the `anon` Postgres role is different from an anonymous user in Supabase Auth. ... Create a `.sql` file under `supabase/tests/` that asserts allow and deny for `select`, `insert`, `update`, and `delete`, for `anon` and `authenticated`. ... Once RLS is enabled, no data is accessible through the API when using a publishable key, until you create policies. ...       not has_table_privilege(\'anon\', \'public.announcements\', \'insert,update,delete\'), ... A function owned by a role without `bypassrls`, or reading a table set to `force row level security`, evaluates the membership policy again and stays recursive.\n\nDatabase\n\n# \n\nRow Level Security\n\nSecure your data using Postgres Row Level Security.\n\nPostgres Row Level Security (RLS) gives you granular authorization rules that run inside the database.\n\nA table in an exposed schema without RLS is readable and writable by any role with a grant on it. Enable RLS on every table in an exposed schema. On projects that still grant `anon` and `authenticated` by default, revoke those grants. Adding policies doesn\'t remove them.\n\nUse the guide in three parts:\n\n  * Understand Row Level Security explains how grants and policies combine to control access.\n  * Secure a table with RLS is the procedure to follow for every table in an exposed schema: grants, policies, and `supabase test db`.\n  * RLS reference documents the helper functions and patterns you use inside a policy expression.\n\nRead the first section when you\'re deciding how to model access. Go directly to the second section when you\'re ready to secure a table.\n\n## Understand Row Level Security#\nCombine RLS with Supabase Auth for end-to-end user security from the browser to the database.\n\n### Grants and policies#\n\nPostgres runs two checks before a client touches a table. Grants decide whether a role can run an operation on the table at all. Policies decide which rows that operation applies to. Set both for every table you expose.\n\nOn existing projects, a new table in `public` starts with every privilege already granted to all three roles:\n\nRole  | Granted automatically  | What it should keep\n--- | --- | ---\n`anon`  | `select`, `insert`, `update`, `delete`  | Only what signed-out visitors are meant to read\n`authenticated`  | `select`, `insert`, `update`, `delete`  | Only the operations your app exposes to signed-in users\n`service_role`  | `select`, `insert`, `update`, `delete`  | Full access. It bypasses RLS, so keep it server-side\n\nAdding policies doesn\'t take those grants back. A table protected only by policies still hands `anon` an insert path if you never revoke the grant.\n\nNot every project grants these automatically. See Default privileges. Grant each role only the operations it needs.\n\nA missing grant raises a `42501` error before any policy runs. When a request fails that your policy should allow, check the grants before you change the policy. To set them, see Enable RLS and set the grants.\n\n### Authenticated and unauthenticated roles#\n\nSupabase maps every request to one of the roles:\n\n  * `anon`: an unauthenticated request (the user is not signed in)\n  * `authenticated`: an authenticated request (the user is signed in)\n\nThese are Postgres Roles. You can use these roles within your Policies using the `TO` clause:\n    \n    `\n    \n    1\n    \n    create policy "Profiles are viewable by everyone"\n    \n    2\n    \n    on profiles for select\n    \n    3\n    \n    to authenticated, anon\n    \n    4\n    \n    using ( true );\n    \n    5\n    \n    6\n    \n    -- OR\n    \n    7\n    \n    8\n    \n    create policy "Public profiles are viewable only by authenticated users"\n    \n    9\n    \n    on profiles for select\n    \n    10\n    \n    to authenticated\n    \n    11\n    \n    using ( true );\n    \n    `\n\nAnonymous user vs the anon key\n\nUsing the `anon` Postgres role is different from an anonymous user in Supabase Auth. An anonymous user assumes the `authenticated` role to access the database and can be differentiated from a permanent user by checking the `is_anonymous` claim in the JWT.\n\nA policy that reads `to anon using ( true )` grants every unauthenticated visitor read access to every row the role can already reach through grants. Use it only for data that is meant to be public.\n\n### Views and RLS#\n\nViews bypass RLS by default because they are usually created with the `postgres` user. This is a feature of Postgres, which automatically creates views with `security definer`. A view over a protected table hands out every row its policies were meant to withhold, so a view needs the same attention as a table. To create one safely, see Expose a view safely.\n\n## Secure a table with RLS#\n\nFollow these steps for every table in an exposed schema:\n\n  1. Enable RLS and set the grants to match the app.\n  2. Write a policy per operation.\n  3. Create a `.sql` file under `supabase/tests/` that asserts allow and deny for `select`, `insert`, `update`, and `delete`, for `anon` and `authenticated`.\n  4. Run `supabase test db` and fix what it reports.\n\nUntil the suite passes, you don\'t know whether the policies do what you intended.\n\n### Enable RLS and set the grants#\n\nRun these statements in the SQL Editor for a one-off change, or in a migration to keep the change reproducible across environments. Grants and RLS belong in the same migration.\n\nEnable RLS, then set the grants to match what each role does in your app:\n\n  1. Enable RLS on the table.\n    \n    `\n    \n    1\n    \n    alter table public.reports enable row level security;\n    \n    `\n\nOnce RLS is enabled, no data is accessible through the API when using a publishable key, until you create policies.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nAPI keys | Supabase Docs (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/getting-started/api-keys)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search2\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 days ago; Crawled: 3 days ago; Running `supabase start` prints a publishable key and a secret key for your local project. ... Every key resolves to a built-in Postgres role, and that role is what your Row Level Security policies match on. ... `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` still exist in the runtime, but they carry the legacy keys, so reading them keeps you on the deprecated path. ...   * A web page, a public document, source code, or a bundled mobile, desktop, or CLI package. ...   * Send publishable and secret keys on the `apikey` header, not on `Authorization: [REDACTED] Migrating to new API keys for the full process.\n\n### Publishable keys and public components#\n\nPublishable keys identify the public components of your application. Public components run in environments where you can\'t keep a secret. These include:\n\n  * Web pages, where the key is bundled in source code.\n  * Mobile or desktop applications, where the key is bundled inside the compiled packages or executables.\n  * CLI, scripts, tools, or other pre-built executables.\n  * Public APIs that return the key without requiring authorization first.\n\nThese environments are always considered public because anyone can retrieve the key from the source code or build artifacts.\n\n### Postgres roles and Row Level Security#\n\nEvery key resolves to a built-in Postgres role, and that role is what your Row Level Security policies match on. Using a publishable key doesn\'t mean your user is anonymous: your application authenticates with the publishable key while your user authenticates separately through Supabase Auth with their own JWT.\n\nKey  | User signed in through Supabase Auth  | Postgres role\n--- | --- | ---\nPublishable key  | No  | `anon`\nPublishable key  | Yes  | `authenticated`\nSecret key  | Not applicable  | `service_role`\n\nWrite your Row Level Security policies for the `anon` and `authenticated` roles. Policies never apply to a secret key, because `service_role` has the `BYPASSRLS` attribute.\n\nPostgres evaluates table grants first, and only then applies Row Level Security. The two failures look different. A missing grant returns a permission error, including for `service_role`, whereas a policy that matches no rows returns an empty result. Check the grant before you debug the policy. See Securing your API.\n\n### Secret keys and elevated access#\n\nUnlike publishable keys, secret keys allow elevated access to your project\'s data. Use them only in secure, developer-controlled components of your application, such as:\n\n  * Servers that run their own authorization checks, such as Edge Functions, microservices, and web servers.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nEnvironment variables | Supabase Docs (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/secrets)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search3\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 days ago; Crawled: 3 days ago;   * `SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEYS`: The `publishable` keys JSON dictionary for your Supabase API. ...   * `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`: The `anon` key for your Supabase API. ... This key bypasses Row Level Security\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nSecuring your API | Supabase Docs (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/api/securing-your-api)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search4\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 days ago; Crawled: 3 days ago; The internal `supabase_admin` role grants them to `anon`, `authenticated`, and `service_role`, but it can\'t authenticate through the Data API. ...       "hint": "Grant the required privileges to the current role with: GRANT SELECT ON public.your_table TO anon;" ... # AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*
